
❯ # Implement Campaign Content Generation & Approval

  Read `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and inspect the existing
  segmentation workflow, Go backend, PostgreSQL schema, and Vue components.

  We have a working flow where a marketer submits a natural-language brief,
  Claude suggests audience segments, and the user selects one.

  Now implement the next two steps: **AI content generation and human
  review/approval.**

  Keep the implementation simple and consistent with the existing architecture.

  ## 1. Initial Content Generation

  Once the user selects an audience, generate campaign content using Claude.

  Provide Claude with:
  - Original campaign brief
  - Selected audience and its targeting conditions
  - Relevant campaign context and requirements
  - Channel constraints and supported personalization variables

  Claude should generate:

  - SMS body (maximum 160 characters)
  - Email subject (maximum 80 characters)
  - Email body
  - Campaign settings inferred from the brief:
    - Tone (friendly, professional, playful, premium, urgent, etc.)
    - Occasion (welcome, cart recovery, win-back, promotion, etc.)
    - Length (short, balanced, detailed)
    - CTA (shop now, complete purchase, explore, etc.)

  Use structured output for the content and settings.

  **Stream the generated content to the Vue interface as it arrives.** Do not
  simulate streaming by delaying a completed response.

  SMS and email must be separate, independently editable templates.

  Generate both channels by default, but allow the user to enable or disable
  either channel.

  Preserve campaign facts from the brief. Do not invent discounts, expiration
  dates, coupon codes, or promotional promises.

  ## 2. Draft Review & Regeneration

  After generation, display **Draft 1**.

  The marketer should be able to:
  - Review SMS and email separately.
  - Edit SMS body, email subject, and email body directly.
  - Change the generated tone, occasion, length, and CTA settings.
  - Provide optional free-text feedback.
  - Preview personalized messages using a few actual synthetic customers from
  the selected audience.
  - Regenerate the content using the current draft, updated settings, and
  feedback.

  Regeneration produces **Draft 2**, preserving Draft 1 so the user can compare
  or return to it.

  Keep this simple: no separate AI rewriting system, multiple generation modes,
  or complex version management.

  Support further regenerations using the same mechanism if necessary.

  Do not overwrite the current draft while new content is streaming. Replace it
  only when generation completes successfully.

  ## 3. Content Validation

  Enforce the required constraints in the Go backend:

  - SMS body: maximum 160 characters.
  - Email subject: maximum 80 characters.
  - Required content fields must not be empty for enabled channels.
  - Only supported personalization variables, initially `{{first_name}}`.
  - Validate personalized SMS length after variable substitution, not only the
  raw template.
  - Revalidate content after manual edits and before approval.

  Show character counts and clear validation feedback in Vue.

  Keep validation straightforward and testable without an actual Claude API
  key.

  ## 4. Campaign Persistence & Approval

  Persist the campaign with:
  - Original brief
  - Selected audience rules
  - Generated campaign settings
  - SMS and email templates
  - Enabled channels
  - Draft versions
  - Campaign status (`draft`, `approved`)
  - Approval timestamp

  Store only what is needed for this workflow. Avoid unnecessary tables or
  abstractions.

  The marketer must explicitly approve the final draft.

  On approval:
  - Revalidate the selected audience and content.
  - Save the approved campaign snapshot.
  - Prevent further modification of the approved version.
  - Clearly indicate that approval is complete.

  **Approval must not trigger message delivery yet.** Queueing and mock
  providers will be implemented separately.

  ## 5. Frontend Experience

  Extend the existing Vue workflow rather than redesigning the application.

  The intended flow is:

  **Brief → Audience Selection → Content Generation → Draft Review → Approve**

  The content editor should provide:
  - Separate SMS and email sections.
  - Streaming generation states.
  - Automatically populated campaign settings.
  - Editable content and settings.
  - Optional feedback for regeneration.
  - Simple Draft 1 / Draft 2 navigation.
  - Personalized recipient previews.
  - Clear validation and approval states.

  Preserve user edits when switching between channels.

  Keep the UI clean, responsive, and consistent with the current design.

  ## 6. Testing & Verification

  Reuse the existing Anthropic integration and mockable LLM approach.

  Add focused tests for:
  - Structured content generation and parsing
  - Content length and placeholder validation
  - Draft regeneration and preservation
  - Manual edits and approval validation
  - Prevention of modifications after approval
  - API error and interrupted streaming behavior

  Verify streaming with a real Claude call if the environment is configured.

  Use the existing execution tracing approach where useful, recording
  generation inputs, outputs, token usage, latency, and errors without exposing
  secrets.

  Follow `CLAUDE.md`:
  - Do not restart or rebuild Docker services unnecessarily.
  - Do not run repeated full test suites.
  - Avoid browser automation and screenshots unless requested.
  - Do not add unrelated features or infrastructure.
  - Keep changes focused and reviewable.

  ## Scope Boundaries

  Do not implement:
  - Message queues or delivery workers
  - Mock SMS/email providers
  - Scheduling or A/B testing
  - Brand management systems
  - Complex template builders
  - Additional agents or orchestration frameworks
  - Unnecessary dependencies

  Use the simplest implementation that satisfies the case requirements.

  After implementation, briefly report the architecture decisions, API changes,
  tests performed, and any remaining limitations.

  **Goal:** A marketer selects an audience, watches Claude generate SMS and
  email content, adjusts settings or provides feedback to create a second
  draft, and explicitly approves the final version. Nothing is sent yet.

  ⎿  Plan saved to:
     ~/.claude/plans/pasted-content-id-bfc4-implement-cheerful-turtle.md · /plan
     to edit
     Campaign content generation, draft review & approval

     Context

     Today the flow ends when the marketer clicks "Use this audience"
     (NewCampaignView.vue (frontend/src/views/NewCampaignView.vue)): nothing is
     persisted, and the Content and Review steps are shown as "Coming soon".
     This change adds the next two steps: Claude streams SMS and email drafts
     plus inferred settings, the marketer edits them, can regenerate (Draft 2,
     3, …) and must explicitly approve. Campaigns, drafts and the approval
     snapshot are stored in PostgreSQL. Nothing is sent or queued.

     Flow: Brief → Audience → Content (stream Draft 1) → Review/edit/regenerate
     → Approve.

     Key decisions

     - New package internal/content (generator, validation, service). It mirrors
       internal/segmentation: a Generator interface with Claude and Mock
       implementations, strict parsing, a Service over a pgx pool, and a trace
       row for every run.
     - Small shared internal/llm package. It moves the error values
       (ErrAuth/ErrRetry/ErrRefusal/ErrBadReply), the *anthropic.Error →
       typed-error mapping and client construction out of
       segmentation/generator.go (backend/internal/segmentation/generator.go).
       Segmentation keeps its names as aliases (ErrAuth = llm.ErrAuth), so its
       code and tests don't change. Both features use the same ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
       / ANTHROPIC_MODEL and the same default model.
     - Real streaming. client.Messages.NewStreaming with OutputConfig.Format set
       to a JSON schema; we accumulate with message.Accumulate(event) to get
       the final usage and stop_reason. Each text delta is appended to a buffer.
       A small tolerant partial-JSON string extractor (partial.go) pulls the
       current value of sms_body, email_subject, email_body and the four
       settings out of the unfinished JSON. The handler emits an SSE field event
       whenever a value changes. The schema puts the settings first, then SMS,
       subject and email body, so they fill in that order.
     - The mock streams for real too. It builds a deterministic draft from
       keywords in the brief and the current settings, serialises it, and passes
       it to onDelta in chunks with no sleeps. The same extractor and SSE path
       run against it, and no API key is needed.
     - Structured output schema (strict, additionalProperties:false):
       settings{tone, occasion, length, cta} as enums, then sms_body,
       email_subject, email_body. Enums:
       - tone: friendly/professional/playful/premium/urgent/empathetic
       - occasion:
         welcome/cart_recovery/win_back/promotion/reminder/announcement/loyalty
       - length: short/balanced/detailed
       - cta:
         shop_now/complete_purchase/explore/claim_offer/learn_more/come_back
     - Prompt (system prompt, cached prefix): channel limits (SMS ≤160 after
       {{first_name}} substitution, so the prompt gives a budget that leaves
       room for the longest first name in the audience); subject ≤80;
       {{first_name}} is the only variable; plain-text email body; write in the
       brief's language; never invent discounts, codes, dates or promises that
       are not in the brief. The brief is wrapped as untrusted data, as
       segmentation already does. The user message carries the brief, the
       audience name and its conditions_text, eligible counts per channel, and
       the longest first name. For a regeneration it also carries the current
       draft (including the marketer's edits), the selected settings ("use
       exactly these") and the optional feedback.
     - Which generated output is accepted. Structural problems (bad JSON,
       unknown fields, bad enum, an empty required field, refusal, max_tokens)
       mean ErrBadReply, and no draft is saved. Constraint problems (length,
       unsupported placeholder) still become a draft, but it is flagged invalid
       with issues and approval is blocked. The marketer can then edit or
       regenerate instead of losing the output.
     - Validation (validate.go) is pure and needs no DB or API key.
       Validate(draft, maxFirstNameLen) []Issue{Field, Code, Message, Blocking}:
       - at least one channel is enabled;
       - required fields are non-empty for each enabled channel;
       - SMS is ≤160 runes, both raw and after substituting the longest first
         name in the audience;
       - subject is ≤80 runes;
       - placeholders: anything matching {{…}} other than {{first_name}}, plus
         stray {{/}}, is blocking;
       - fact check (warning only): percentages, currency amounts and code-like
         tokens ([A-Z0-9]{4,} that contain a digit) found in the content but not
         in the brief.

       Render(template, customer) does the substitution.
     - Persistence. Two tables and one trace table, added to schema.sql
       (backend/internal/db/schema.sql), idempotent:
       - campaigns(id, created_at, updated_at, brief, audience jsonb {name,
         match, conditions, conditions_text, counts}, status text CHECK IN
         ('draft','approved'), approved_version int, approved_at timestamptz,
         approved_snapshot jsonb)
       - campaign_drafts(id, campaign_id FK, version int, UNIQUE(campaign_id,
         version), created_at, updated_at, source, sms_enabled bool,
         email_enabled bool, settings jsonb, sms_body, email_subject,
         email_body, feedback text)
       - content_runs(id, created_at, campaign_id, from_version, draft_version,
         source, model, status, error, llm_ms, total_ms, input_tokens,
         output_tokens, raw_output (capped), input jsonb). input holds the
         settings, feedback and from_version, never secrets. It reuses the
         statusOf pattern with an extra canceled status.
     - Approval happens in one transaction, SELECT … FOR UPDATE on the campaign:
       a. Reject if the campaign is already approved (409).
       b. Revalidate the audience rules against the current catalog
          (segmentation.Validate) and recount. Every enabled channel must have
          more than 0 eligible recipients.
       c. Re-run Validate on the stored draft with the current longest first
          name.
       d. Write status='approved', approved_version, approved_at and the
          snapshot (audience + counts + draft content + settings).

       After that, every mutation (edit, generate, approve) returns ErrApproved
       → 409. To support the audience step, segmentation gets one exported
       helper, (*Service).CheckAudience(ctx, Alternative) (Counts,
       maxFirstNameLen int, err). It reuses catalog, Validate, Compile and the
       read-only count path, and adds max(length(first_name)) over customers
       eligible for SMS.
     - No overwrite while streaming. A generation creates a new draft row only
       after the stream completes and parses successfully. A client abort or
       stream error saves no draft and records a trace with status
       canceled/unavailable. An in-memory per-campaign mutex rejects a second
       concurrent generation (409). That is enough for a single instance, and it
       is documented.
     - Edits are saved with a debounced PUT (autosave) that returns the server's
       issues. The frontend shows Saving…/Saved honestly and flushes any
       pending save before it regenerates, switches draft or approves.

     API (all under /api/campaigns, new cmd/api/campaigns.go)

     Method/Path: POST /api/campaigns
     Body: {brief, audience:{name, match, conditions, conditions_text}}
     Response: 201 {campaign}. The audience is revalidated and counted on the
       server.
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Method/Path: GET /api/campaigns/{id}
     Body: –
     Response: {campaign, drafts:[{…, issues}]}
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Method/Path: POST /api/campaigns/{id}/generate
     Body: {from_version: 0|n, feedback}
     Response: SSE: field {field, value} … then done {draft, issues} or error
       {message, retryable}. Uses X-Accel-Buffering: no, extends the write
     deadline
       as suggest does, and has a 90s timeout.
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Method/Path: PUT /api/campaigns/{id}/drafts/{version}
     Body: {sms_enabled, email_enabled, settings, sms_body, email_subject,
       email_body}
     Response: {draft, issues}; 409 if approved
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Method/Path: POST /api/campaigns/{id}/approve
     Body: {version}
     Response: {campaign}; 422 {error, issues}; 409 if already approved

     Errors map the same way as the existing suggest handler (auth → 502, busy →
     503, bad reply → 502, refusal → 422). The handler depends on a small
     interface, so it can be tested with a fake. It is wired in main.go
     (backend/cmd/api/main.go) the same way as the segmentation generator
     (Claude if the key is set, otherwise Mock). The SSE writer uses
     http.NewResponseController(w).Flush().

     Frontend

     - api.ts (frontend/src/api.ts): types Campaign, Draft, ContentSettings,
       Issue; createCampaign, getCampaign, saveDraft, approveCampaign;
       streamGeneration(id, body, {onField}, signal), a fetch + ReadableStream
       SSE parser (POST, so EventSource can't be used) that resolves with the
       done payload and throws on error.
     - NewCampaignView.vue (frontend/src/views/NewCampaignView.vue):
       - "Use this audience" calls createCampaign, and the view moves to the
         content stage.
       - The brief composer collapses into a read-only summary of the brief and
         audience.
       - In the stepper, Content and Review become available; current and
         progress are driven by the stage (generating, reviewing, approved).
         Delivery stays "Coming soon".
     - New components/ContentStudio.vue:
       - Owns the campaign/drafts state and starts the Draft 1 stream on mount.
       - Draft tabs (Draft 1 / Draft 2 / …), plus a "Draft N+1 · generating"
         pill while streaming. The live text appears in a separate read-only
         streaming panel, so the current draft is never overwritten. When done
         arrives, the new draft is appended and selected. On an error, the
         current draft stays and a retry action is shown.
       - Channel tabs (SMS | Email) with an enable toggle each. Both editors are
         kept mounted (v-show), so edits are preserved when switching channel.
       - Settings: four selects, filled in from the draft.
       - Feedback textarea and a Regenerate button. Generation always uses the
         currently viewed draft as its base.
       - Approve bar: blocking issues disable it, and approval goes through a
         confirm step ("Approve Draft N? Nothing will be sent yet."). After
         approval it shows a success state, every input is read-only and
         regenerate is hidden.
     - New components/ChannelEditor.vue: fields for one channel; a live
       character counter (raw and personalized worst case for SMS, using the
       max_first_name_len returned with the campaign); per-field issues, with
       server issues authoritative and local counts for instant feedback.
     - New components/RecipientPreview.vue:
       - Up to 3 real synthetic customers from the selected audience (reuses
         previewCustomers / POST /api/segmentation/preview), each rendered with
         {{first_name}} substituted.
       - Shows the per-recipient SMS length, and an "unresolved variable" flag
         if any {{…}} remains.
       - The sample is clearly labelled as such.
     - CampaignsView.vue (frontend/src/views/CampaignsView.vue): only the
       guide's step readiness and the "aren't saved" note change. There is no
       campaign list (out of scope).
     - Styling uses the existing tokens and button styles from style.css
       (frontend/src/style.css) / BriefComposer. The layout follows wireframe
       03: editor on the left, preview on the right, stacked on narrow screens.

     Files

     - New: backend/internal/llm/llm.go; backend/internal/content/{generator.go,
       partial.go, validate.go, service.go, trace.go} plus tests;
       backend/cmd/api/campaigns.go plus test; frontend/src/components/{ContentS
       tudio,ChannelEditor,RecipientPreview}.vue.
     - Modified: segmentation/generator.go (use llm; aliases),
       segmentation/service.go (CheckAudience), db/schema.sql, cmd/api/main.go,
       frontend/src/api.ts, NewCampaignView.vue, CampaignsView.vue (copy only),
       README.md (API and limitations; a short note).

     Tests (focused; DB tests use dbtest.Pool and are skipped without
     DATABASE_URL)

     - content/generator_test.go:
       - strict parse: valid reply, unknown field, bad enum, empty field.
       - Claude streaming against an httptest server (option.WithBaseURL) that
         serves real SSE events:
         - deltas arrive in order, then the final parse and token usage;
         - the server closes the connection mid-stream → error, no result;
         - 401 → ErrAuth, 529 → ErrRetry;
         - stop_reason max_tokens/refusal.
     - content/partial_test.go: incomplete strings, escapes split across chunks,
       \u sequences, fields not started yet.
     - content/validate_test.go: SMS 160 raw vs. personalized, subject 80, empty
       fields only for enabled channels, unknown or malformed placeholders, at
       least one channel, fact-check warnings.
     - content/service_test.go (DB, scripted generator):
       - create → Draft 1;
       - edit + regenerate → Draft 2, and Draft 1 is byte-for-byte unchanged and
         still retrievable;
       - a generator failure or canceled ctx saves no draft and records a trace
         row;
       - invalid content → approve returns issues and the status stays draft;
       - a valid approve stores the snapshot and timestamp;
       - after approval, edit, generate and approve all return ErrApproved.
     - cmd/api/campaigns_test.go: SSE event sequence and an error event using a
       fake service; approve 409/422 mapping.

     Verification

     1. cd backend && go test ./internal/content/... ./internal/segmentation/...
        ./cmd/api/.... If DATABASE_URL isn't set locally, run the DB tests
        inside the running dev backend container with docker compose exec
        backend go test ./... (no restart or rebuild).
     2. cd frontend && npm run type-check && npm run build-only (or the same via
        docker compose exec frontend).
     3. If ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is configured in the running backend, check real
        streaming with curl -N -X POST
        localhost:8080/api/campaigns/{id}/generate after creating a campaign
        with curl, and confirm that field events arrive incrementally before
        done. Then check the trace row in content_runs (tokens and latency are
        recorded, no secrets). Air hot-reloads the backend; services are not
        restarted.
     4. No browser automation or screenshots.

     Known limitations (go in the README)

     - SMS length is counted in Unicode characters. GSM-7 vs UCS-2 segment
       encoding (Turkish characters) is not modelled.
     - In-progress campaigns can't be resumed after a page reload: there is no
       campaign route or list yet, although the data is persisted.
     - The per-campaign generation lock is in-memory (single instance).
     - The fact check is a heuristic warning, not a guarantee.
     - Approval does not trigger delivery; that is a separate milestone.

⏺ The content generation, draft review and approval flow is in place: Brief →
  Audience → Content (streamed) → Review/edit/regenerate → Approve. Approving
  locks the campaign and sends nothing.

  I checked it against real Claude with curl through the running dev backend:
  - Streaming: the first token takes about 4–5 s, then field updates arrive one
    by one. The Vite proxy passes them through without buffering.
  - Draft 1: kept the brief's 30% and added no other offers.
  - Regeneration: used the edited draft and the changed settings, and turned the
    feedback into Draft 2. Draft 1 stayed unchanged.
  - Abort: disconnecting mid-stream saved no draft and was traced as canceled.

  I did not look at the UI in a browser.

  Architecture decisions
  - New internal/content package, built like segmentation: a generator interface
    with Claude and mock versions, strict parsing, a service and a trace table.
  - New internal/llm package for the shared client setup and error mapping.
    Segmentation keeps its error names as aliases, so its code and tests are
    unchanged.
  - Streaming: Claude returns structured JSON as it writes. A small parser reads
    the unfinished JSON and sends the current value of each field to Vue as
    server-sent events.
  - Mock generator: streams its output in chunks with no delays, so tests and
    keyless runs use the same path.
  - Drafts: a new draft is saved only after generation finishes and parses. A
    failed or interrupted run never creates or replaces a draft.
  - Validation is in Go:
    - SMS ≤ 160 characters after substituting the longest first name among
      reachable audience members;
    - email subject ≤ 80;
    - required fields for each enabled channel, and at least one channel;
    - {{first_name}} is the only allowed variable.

    Unparseable model output is rejected and not stored. A well-formed draft
    that breaks a length or variable rule is kept but flagged, and approval is
    blocked until it's fixed.
  - Invented facts: discounts, amounts or codes that aren't in the brief produce
    a warning only. The marketer's feedback also counts as a source of facts.
  - Tables: campaigns, campaign_drafts and content_runs.
  - Approval: in one transaction, it re-checks the audience rules and
    per-channel reach, re-validates the content, then stores a snapshot. After
    that, edits, regeneration and re-approval all return 409.

  API
  - POST /api/campaigns creates a campaign from the brief and audience rules.
  - GET /api/campaigns/{id} returns the campaign and all its drafts.
  - POST /api/campaigns/{id}/generate streams events: field, then done or error.
  - PUT /api/campaigns/{id}/drafts/{version} saves edits (autosaved from the
    UI).
  - POST /api/campaigns/{id}/approve approves a draft version.

  On the frontend, the new components are ContentStudio.vue
  (frontend/src/components/ContentStudio.vue), ChannelEditor.vue
  (frontend/src/components/ChannelEditor.vue) and RecipientPreview.vue
  (frontend/src/components/RecipientPreview.vue), wired into NewCampaignView.vue
  (frontend/src/views/NewCampaignView.vue).

  Tests
  - Parsing the model's reply, including unknown fields, invalid settings and
    empty fields.
  - Claude streaming against a local fake server: normal stream, connection
    closed mid-stream, max-tokens and refusal stops, and 401/429/529 errors.
  - The partial-JSON parser.
  - Content validation rules.
  - The full campaign lifecycle against Postgres in a throwaway schema.
  - The HTTP event sequence and status codes.

  The content, segmentation and cmd/api Go packages pass; frontend type-check
  and build pass.

  Fixed along the way: POST /api/segmentation/preview always failed with
  "reasoning must be 10-600 characters", because the frontend sends only the
  rules. I applied the same one-line fix there, since the recipient preview
  depends on it.

  Remaining limitations (also in the README)
  - There's no campaign list or URL, so an in-progress campaign can't be resumed
    after a page reload, even though it's saved.
  - Only one generation per campaign can run at a time, enforced in memory,
    which works with a single API instance only.
  - SMS length counts Unicode characters; GSM-7 vs. UCS-2 encoding (e.g. Turkish
    letters) and multi-part SMS aren't modelled.
  - The invented-facts check is a heuristic warning, not a guarantee.

  The curl checks left one test campaign (id 1, Drafts 1–5) and its trace rows
  in the dev database. Nothing is committed.

❯ # Smart Campaign Composer — Advanced Campaign Composition

  ## Context & Objective

  Read `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`, and inspect the existing Go
  backend, PostgreSQL schema, Claude integration, and Vue frontend before
  implementation.

  We already have a campaign workflow built around:

  **Brief → Audience Segmentation → Content Generation → Draft Review →
  Approval**

  The existing Standard experience is the foundation. Do not replace or
  redesign it.

  We now want to extend this experience with:

  1. Persistent, unlimited campaign drafts
  2. Optional A/B/n variants, with a maximum of 3 active variants
  3. Behavioral group personalization
  4. Individual customer personalization
  5. Automatic localization based on recipient language
  6. Transparent AI generation costs
  7. Minimal audience-message consistency checks
  8. Appropriate campaign and variant-level delivery monitoring

  The goal is to build a coherent marketing product, not a generic
  experimentation platform or an overly sophisticated personalization engine.

  Keep the implementation pragmatic, integrated with our existing architecture,
  and proportional to this time-boxed case study.

  ---

  ## 1. Core Concept: Drafts and Variants Are Different

  This distinction is essential.

  ### Campaign Drafts

  A draft is an editable version of campaign content.

  Claude initially generates Draft 1 from the campaign brief and selected
  audience. The marketer can modify its content, adjust generation settings,
  provide feedback, or request another generation.

  Each regeneration creates another draft.

  Users should also be able to create drafts explicitly, without requiring AI
  generation.

  There is **no product-imposed limit on the number of drafts**.

  Drafts must remain available. Regenerating or creating another draft must
  never silently overwrite or delete an earlier draft.

  Each draft contains:

  - SMS template
  - Email subject
  - Email body
  - Generated or user-adjusted settings such as tone, occasion, length, and CTA
  - Enabled channels
  - Basic origin information, such as initial generation, regeneration, manual
  creation, or duplication

  The user can:

  - Navigate between drafts
  - Edit an existing draft
  - Regenerate from a chosen draft
  - Duplicate a draft
  - Create a blank draft
  - Preview and compare drafts
  - Select a draft for approval or promotion to an experiment variant

  Avoid introducing complex revision histories inside individual drafts. Drafts
  themselves provide the necessary working history.

  ### Campaign Variants

  A va
  ──── (607 lines hidden) ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  d content.

  ### Localization

  - Recipient language selects the correct template.
  - Missing language values have a defined fallback.
  - Localization preserves required campaign facts.
  - SMS length and template validation are applied to localized content.

  ### Monitoring

  - Variant and group identifiers remain attached to delivery records.
  - Retries do not change experiment assignment.
  - Generation metrics are not confused with delivery metrics.
  - Dashboard aggregates are consistent with stored delivery records.

  Use deterministic/mock testing for backend behavior. Make selective real
  Claude calls to verify semantic generation quality.

  Avoid extensive testing infrastructure or repetitive full-system checks.

  ---

  ## 11. Constraints and Working Style

  This is a time-boxed engineering case.

  Prioritize a coherent, reliable implementation over feature breadth.

  Before coding, inspect the existing implementation and identify anything that
  is already supported so we avoid duplication.

  Resolve routine engineering decisions independently.

  If a proposed detail introduces disproportionate complexity, choose the
  simpler behavior while preserving the product requirements described above.

  Do not introduce:

  - Additional autonomous AI agents
  - Generic experimentation frameworks
  - Statistical optimization engines
  - Complex customer analytics infrastructure
  - External queues unless genuinely necessary
  - Additional databases
  - Unnecessary dependencies
  - Large unrelated refactors

  Follow `CLAUDE.md`:

  - Do not restart or rebuild running services without permission.
  - Do not stop existing processes.
  - Do not clear database state.
  - Do not perform repeated full test runs.
  - Do not run browser automation or screenshots unless requested.
  - Keep verification proportional to the changes.
  - Stop once the requested work and relevant checks are complete.

  ## Expected Outcome

  A marketer can:

  1. Generate a Standard campaign from a brief and selected audience.
  2. Create, edit, regenerate, duplicate, and retain unlimited drafts.
  3. Optionally promote up to three drafts into an A/B experiment.
  4. Alternatively personalize the campaign by behavioral groups or individual
  customers.
  5. Preview appropriately localized SMS and email content.
  6. Understand additional AI generation costs before applying expensive
  personalization.
  7. Review the exact campaign configuration before approval.
  8. Monitor subsequent generation and delivery using appropriate campaign,
  variant, or group breakdowns.

  ⎿  Plan saved to:
     ~/.claude/plans/pasted-content-id-bfc4-implement-cheerful-turtle.md · /plan
     to edit
     Advanced campaign composition: drafts, A/B variants, localization,
     personalization, monitoring contract

     Context

     The Standard flow already works end to end (uncommitted working tree; see
     backend/internal/content, ContentStudio.vue): Brief → Audience → streamed
     Draft 1 → edit/regenerate (Drafts 2…n) → validated approval. This task
     extends it with:
     - manual and duplicated drafts;
     - an optional A/B/n experiment (at most 3 variants);
     - mandatory localization by recipient language;
     - two alternative personalization modes: Behavioral Groups and Individual
       AI personalization;
     - visible AI cost;
     - a minimal deterministic audience–message consistency gate;
     - a recipient resolver plus a delivery-record contract that a future
       delivery pipeline consumes for variant and group monitoring.

     Standard stays the default and keeps working throughout.

     Decisions agreed with the user:
     - The seed and existing data get non-tr languages.
     - Phase 5 is limited to the resolver plus the delivery-record contract; the
       delivery worker and dashboard come later.
     - All phases are implemented in one pass.

     What already exists and is reused

     - Drafts: campaign_drafts with unlimited versions, Generate (streaming,
       never overwrites), SaveDraft, Approve + snapshot, and Validate (lengths
       after {{first_name}}, placeholders, facts warning) in
       content/{service,validate}.go.
     - Streaming: the partialStrings field extractor and SSE writer
       (cmd/api/campaigns.go).
     - Tracing: content_runs (tokens, latency, status) and llm.ClassifyError.
     - Audience rules: segmentation.Condition/Alternative, Validate, Compile,
       Normalize, Describe, CheckAudience and customers.StatsCTE. These also
       define behavioral groups.
     - Seed: language logic in seed/generate.go (Language: "tr", plus en at
       10%/30%).

     Phase 0: language data

     - seed/generate.go: about 70% tr, 25% en (higher in cosmopolitan cities),
       and about 5% de. de is deliberately unsupported, to demonstrate the
       fallback.
     - seed.BackfillLanguages(ctx, pool) runs from setupDatabase and is
       idempotent. It only acts when no customer has a non-tr language. It
       assigns languages deterministically from a hash of the customer id
       (abs(hashtext(id::text)) % 100) with the same proportions, and changes
       only the language column.
     - Supported content languages: tr, en. An unsupported or unknown language
       falls back to en. This is explicit and documented.

     Phase 1: drafts and variants

     Schema (idempotent ALTER … ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS / CREATE TABLE IF NOT
     EXISTS):
     - campaign_drafts:
       - origin: initial | regenerate | candidate | manual | duplicate
       - parent_version
       - language: the base language of the draft's own fields
       - translations jsonb: {lang: {sms_body, email_subject, email_body,
         base_hash}}
     - campaigns:
       - personalization: standard | groups | individual (default standard)
       - selected_version
       - base_language
       - languages text[]
     - campaign_variants(campaign_id, position 1..3, draft_version), primary key
       (campaign_id, position), unique (campaign_id, draft_version). Labels
       A/B/C come from position. Removing a variant compacts the positions in
       one transaction. A/B is enabled when the table has rows.

     Service (content/drafts.go, content/variants.go):
     - CreateDraft(id, {from_version?}):
       - With no source, it creates a blank draft (manual): settings copied from
         the selected draft, empty texts.
       - With a source, it creates a duplicate, copying content and
         translations.
     - Generate gains intent: regenerate | candidate. A candidate's prompt
       receives the active variants' texts and is told to use "a meaningfully
       different message strategy (angle, structure, hook) with the same facts,
       offer and constraints". The result is a normal draft with
       origin=candidate. Still one candidate per call.
     - SelectDraft(version) sets the Standard selection.
     - SetVariants(versions []int), used for enable, replace, remove and
       disable:
       - 0 versions, or 2 to 3 versions, are accepted;
       - every version must exist;
       - no duplicates;
       - 409 if the campaign is approved.

       A variant references its draft, so editing Draft 4 while it is Variant B
       edits Variant B, and both are labelled the same in the UI. No unrelated
       draft can affect a variant. Approval snapshots the content.
     - Personalization modes and A/B are mutually exclusive.
       SetPersonalization(mode) is rejected while variants exist (and the
       reverse); the UI explains this. Changing mode never deletes drafts,
       groups or personalized messages.
     - Assignment: variantFor(campaignID, customerID, n) = the first 8 bytes of
       sha256("campaign:customer") mod n. It is pure, stable, and gives one
       variant per customer for all channels. Counts per variant are shown in
       the experiment panel (equal allocation: 50/50 or thirds).

     Phase 2: localization

     - CheckAudience also returns reachable members per language (unsupported
       languages counted under en). The campaign stores languages and
       base_language, the most common one.
     - The draft prompt changes from "the brief's language" to "write in
       {base_language}".
     - Automatic translation:
       - After a generated draft is stored, the same SSE request runs a localize
         call (non-streaming): one call for all other represented languages,
         with a schema of versions[{language, sms_body, email_subject,
         email_body}]. The prompt keeps facts, offer, variables and channel
         limits.
       - SSE events: localizing, then done (the draft with translations).
       - If localization fails, the draft is still kept and the translations are
         marked missing (Retry button).
     - Manual, duplicate and edited drafts show stale or missing translations
       (base_hash ≠ hash of the current base) and get an Update translations
       button. Translations can be edited directly through PUT /drafts/{v},
       which accepts translations.
     - Validation runs per language version.
     - Previews pick customers per language, with a language switch in
       RecipientPreview.

     Phase 3: behavioral groups

     - Segmentation additions:
       - (*Service).AudienceProfile(ctx, a) returns aggregates over the
         reachable audience: size, distributions of order_count, total_spent
         quartiles, days since last order/cart/active, cities, languages,
         categories.
       - CompileGroups(audience, groups []Alternative, now) reuses
         compileCondition with shared binds and builds CASE WHEN g1 THEN 1 …
         ELSE 0 END, where 0 is the remainder group "Everyone else". The groups
         are therefore mutually exclusive and cover the audience by
         construction.
       - (*Service).GroupMembers(ctx, a, groups, fn) iterates members with their
         group index and attributes, for sizes, examples and the resolver.
       - The exported ConditionSchema() and field guide are shared with the
         audience prompt.
     - Propose (POST /campaigns/{id}/groups/propose):
       - One Claude call with the profile and the base draft. The reply schema
         is {outcome: groups|not_meaningful, message, groups[1..9]{name,
         description, match, conditions}}.
       - Each group goes through Normalize/Validate; invalid or empty groups are
         dropped, with a warning.
       - The remainder group is added when it has members, for at most 10 groups
         in total.
       - The result is stored in campaign_groups(campaign_id, position, name,
         description, match, conditions jsonb, size, languages text[], content
         jsonb {lang: {...}, base_hash}, status pending|generated|failed,
         error). Re-proposing replaces unapproved groups (confirmed in the UI).
       - not_meaningful is shown with its explanation, and the marketer can
         continue with Standard.
     - Examples (GET …/groups/{pos}/examples): a typical member (median spend),
       a high-value/most active member, and a sparse-history member, with their
       real attributes (orders, spend, last order/cart, city, language,
       consents).
     - Estimate, shown before generating: number of calls = number of groups
       that have members; tokens are the average of earlier runs, or defaults;
       the dollar amount uses a price table.
     - Generate (POST …/groups/generate): a background job with concurrency 3.
       Each group gets one call producing SMS and email for only that group's
       languages, using the base draft as context. Progress is visible through
       GET campaign. Group content can be edited (PUT …/groups/{pos}) and is
       validated like drafts.

     Phase 4: individual personalization

     - Table: personalized_messages(campaign_id, customer_id, language, status
       pending|generated|fallback|failed, sms_body, email_subject, email_body,
       reason, input_tokens, output_tokens, updated_at), primary key
       (campaign_id, customer_id).
     - History (segmentation/customers query): first name, language, city, order
       count, last 5 orders (category, relative days, amount), recent cart
       categories. Email, phone and last name are never sent. "Sufficient
       history" means at least one order, or a cart in the last 180 days.
       Otherwise the customer is a fallback without an LLM call.
     - Sample (POST …/individual/sample {customer_id}): a synchronous call in
       the customer's language, with the base draft as context. It returns the
       content, the exact data used, usage, and an estimate for the remaining
       recipients (count, calls, tokens and USD, with an "estimate" notice). The
       sample is stored and reused.
     - Apply to all (POST …/individual/apply, with explicit confirmation):
       - Inserts pending rows for reachable members, plus fallback rows, then
         starts an in-process runner and returns 202 immediately.
       - The runner uses the server lifetime context, a 4-wide semaphore, and
         one retry for ErrRetry. Per message, Validate runs with that customer's
         name length. Any blocking issue, or any unverified fact, results in
         failed with a reason. Nothing invalid is stored as usable content;
         failed customers fall back to the Standard template.
       - POST …/individual/stop stops the run. Apply again resumes it
         (idempotent, processes pending rows).
     - Progress (generated, failed, fallback, pending) and actual cost appear in
       GET campaign. A sample of outputs is listed for review.

     Cost (all modes)

     - llm.Price(model) holds USD per million tokens: Haiku 5.5 $0.10/$0.50,
       Sonnet 5.5 $2/$10, Opus 5.5 $4/$20. An unknown model shows tokens only.
     - content_runs gets kind (draft | localize | groups | group_content |
       individual), customer_id and group_position. Every call is traced there.
     - Actual cost per campaign is the sum of content_runs. Estimates use the
       average tokens of the same kind and model, with fixed defaults when there
       is no history. AI cost is labelled separately from delivery cost.

     Consistency gate (deterministic, content/gate.go)

     Gate(config, audience) []Issue, used by approval and by the review screen.

     Blocking:
     - existing Validate for every unit in use: the selected draft or each
       variant, × each language, plus each group × language;
     - missing translations for represented languages;
     - A/B with fewer than 2 or more than 3 variants;
     - groups without generated content, or failed groups;
     - individual generation still pending or running;
     - enabled channel without reach (existing).

     Warnings:
     - stale translations or group content (the base changed after they were
       written);
     - individual failures and fallbacks (counts);
     - first-purchase wording (first order|first purchase|ilk sipariş|ilk
       alışveriş) while the audience contains prior buyers (with count);
     - "welcome back / missed you / tekrar" wording while the audience contains
       customers who never ordered;
     - facts not in the brief (existing).

     No LLM verification.

     Approval (POST /approve {version?}):
     - version selects the Standard draft; it is ignored when variants exist.
     - The gate is re-checked and the audience recounted.
     - The snapshot is frozen: mode, languages, selected draft or variants
       (content + translations), groups (definitions, content, sizes),
       individual summary (counts and cost). Per-recipient individual rows
       become immutable because every mutation checks the approved status.
     - Nothing is sent.

     Phase 5: resolver and monitoring contract

     - content/resolve.go: resolveRecipient(customer, config) []Message is pure.
       For each consented channel it chooses:
       - the content source: individual (generated) → group → variant/selected
         draft;
       - the language: the customer's language if supported and present,
         otherwise en, otherwise the base language;
       - the final text, after rendering {{first_name}}.

       Each message carries variant_position, group_position, language and
       content_source (individual | group | template | fallback).
     - (*Service).RecipientPlan(ctx, id) streams members through GroupMembers
       and returns counts by variant, group, language, channel and source. The
       review screen shows it.
     - Delivery contract: a deliveries table with campaign_id, customer_id,
       channel, variant_position, group_position, language, content_source,
       subject, body, status queued|sending|sent|delivered|failed, attempts,
       last_error, timestamps and UNIQUE(campaign_id, customer_id, channel).
       Assignment is computed once at materialization, so retries never change
       it. A small delivery.Breakdown(ctx, campaignID) query groups by
       variant/group × channel × status, plus retry totals, labelled "delivery
       performance". It is tested with inserted rows. There is no worker or
       dashboard yet (delivery milestone).

     API additions (cmd/api/campaigns.go; same error mapping, 409 after
     approval)

     - POST /campaigns/{id}/drafts {from_version?}
     - POST /campaigns/{id}/drafts/{v}/localize
     - PUT /campaigns/{id}/selection {version}
     - PUT /campaigns/{id}/variants {versions:[…]}
     - PUT /campaigns/{id}/personalization {mode}
     - POST …/groups/propose, GET …/groups/{pos}/examples, POST
       …/groups/generate, PUT …/groups/{pos}
     - GET …/audience/members?limit= (sample picker), POST …/individual/sample,
       POST …/individual/apply, POST …/individual/stop
     - GET /campaigns/{id}/review (gate issues + recipient plan + cost)
     - generate gains intent and the localizing event.
     - GET /campaigns/{id} adds variants, personalization, groups, individual
       progress, cost, languages and base_language.

     The new LLM calls go on the generator: Translate, ProposeGroups,
     GroupContent, Personalize. Claude implements them with a shared
     non-streaming structured complete() helper; Mock implements deterministic
     versions so everything runs without a key.

     Frontend (extend, not redesign)

     - src/campaign.ts: a small reactive store (useCampaign) holding the view
       and its actions, so the panels share state without prop drilling.
       ContentStudio moves onto it.
     - ContentStudio.vue:
       - draft toolbar: New draft, Duplicate, Compare with… (a read-only
         side-by-side of another draft);
       - origin chips (Generated / Regenerated / Candidate / Manual / Copy of
         Draft n);
       - "Variant B" badges on drafts;
       - language tabs in the editor (base + translations, with stale/missing
         badges, Update translations);
       - the regenerate panel stays as it is.
     - New ExperimentPanel.vue:
       - an A/B toggle (the current draft becomes A);
       - slots A/B/C, each with a draft picker, "Use current draft" and Remove;
       - Generate candidate, which streams a new draft and then offers "Make it
         Variant B/C";
       - the allocation (50/50 or thirds) with assigned counts;
       - a note that delivery metrics are not conversion metrics;
       - a disabled state with an explanation when a personalization mode is
         active.
     - New PersonalizationPanel.vue: three option cards (Standard default;
       Behavioral groups; Individual) explaining one template vs. a few group
       templates vs. hundreds of unique messages, with expected cost. It
       contains:
       - GroupsPanel.vue: propose → a list of groups (name, description, size,
         languages, conditions text) → representative previews per group (3
         profiles with real attributes, language switch) → cost estimate →
         Generate group messages → per-group editors (reuse ChannelEditor) with
         progress;
       - IndividualPanel.vue: a customer picker → Generate sample → the output
         next to the exact data used → an estimate box (recipients, calls,
         tokens, USD, "final cost may differ") → Apply to all confirmation → a
         progress bar (generated, failed, fallback, pending), Stop/Resume, and
         sample outputs.
     - New ReviewPanel.vue, which replaces the confirm step in the approve bar.
       It summarizes:
       - the audience and eligibility;
       - channels;
       - the personalization mode;
       - the selected draft or variants with their allocation;
       - languages;
       - the recipient plan counts;
       - representative previews;
       - AI generation status and cost (actual so far plus pending estimates);
       - gate issues.

       Approve stays disabled while anything blocking or still running remains.
       The stepper moves to Review while it is open.
     - RecipientPreview.vue gains language selection (samples taken from that
       language's members).

     Tests (focused; DB tests via dbtest, Mock/scripted generators, no API key)

     - Drafts and variants:
       - manual, duplicate and regenerate never alter other drafts;
       - variants come only from explicit promotion; 4 are rejected;
       - replacing and removing compacts the labels;
       - approval freezes the snapshot, and edits or variant changes then
         return 409.
     - Assignment:
       - variantFor is stable across calls;
       - roughly equal split over 3,000 ids;
       - the resolver gives the same variant for SMS and email of one customer.
     - Localization:
       - the resolver picks tr/en, and de falls back to en;
       - translations are validated (an SMS over 160 in en is blocking);
       - missing or stale translations;
       - the mock translation keeps the "30%" fact.
     - Groups:
       - CompileGroups assignment is exclusive and complete: group sizes sum to
         the audience size, order is respected, and the remainder group works;
       - assignment is deterministic;
       - invalid proposed groups are dropped;
       - content is generated only for languages present in each group.
     - Individual:
       - the prompt input contains only allowed fields;
       - no history → fallback without a call;
       - an invented offer → failed, falls back;
       - the runner's peak concurrency stays ≤ 4 (an instrumented fake);
       - stop and resume.
     - Gate: first-purchase and welcome-back warnings, and the blocking rules
       above.
     - Monitoring: Breakdown aggregates match the inserted delivery rows;
       variant and group ids stay on the records; a retry (attempts++) keeps the
       variant.
     - HTTP: new routes map to the right status codes; SSE localizing event
       order.
     - Real Claude (selective): one draft plus localization, one group proposal
       and generation on a small audience, and one individual sample, checked
       for semantic quality and fact preservation.

     Verification

     - go test on ./internal/content/... ./internal/segmentation/...
       ./internal/seed/... ./internal/delivery/... ./cmd/api/... with
       DATABASE_URL pointing at the running Postgres. DB tests use isolated
       schemas.
     - npm run type-check && npm run build-only.
     - curl against the hot-reloaded backend: the backfill produced en/de
       customers; a draft with translations; a group proposal; an individual
       sample; the review plan. No restarts, no browser automation.

     Order and limitations

     Phases 0 → 1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 5, with focused tests after each. These
     limitations go in the README:
     - in-memory job runner (single instance; Apply again resumes after a
       restart);
     - the consistency gate is keyword-based;
     - no delivery worker or monitoring dashboard yet;
     - no conversion metrics or winner selection;
     - de is shown only through the fallback.

         +cally from a                                                   
      12 +// hash of the customer id. It only touches the language column
         + and does                                                      
      13 +// nothing once any customer has a language other than 'tr'.   
      14 +func BackfillLanguages(ctx context.Context, pool *pgxpool.Pool)
         + (int64, error) {                                              
      15 +        cities := make([]string, 0, len(cosmopolitan))         
      16 +        for c := range cosmopolitan {                          
      17 +                cities = append(cities, c)                     
      18 +        }                                                      
      19 +        tag, err := pool.Exec(ctx, fmt.Sprintf(`               
      20 +                WITH h AS (SELECT id, abs(hashtext('lang:' || i
         +d::text)) %% 100 AS p, city = ANY($1) AS cosmo FROM customers) 
      21 +                UPDATE customers c SET language = CASE         
      22 +                        WHEN h.p < %[1]d THEN 'de'             
      23 +                        WHEN h.p < %[1]d + CASE WHEN h.cosmo TH
         +EN %[3]d ELSE %[2]d END THEN 'en'                              
      24 +                        ELSE 'tr' END                          
      25 +                FROM h                                         
      26 +                WHERE c.id = h.id AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM
         + customers WHERE language <> 'tr')`,                           
      27 +                germanShare, englishShare, englishShareCosmo), 
         +cities)                                                        
      28 +        if err != nil {                                        
      29 +                return 0, fmt.Errorf("backfill languages: %w", 
         +err)                                                           
      30 +        }                                                      
      31 +        return tag.RowsAffected(), nil                         
      32 +}

       2 +// (upcoming) delivery pipeline and campaign monitoring. Each r

         +nID int64) ([]Row, error) {                                    
      31 +        ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, 5*time.Second) 
      32 +        defer cancel()                                         
      33 +        rows, err := pool.Query(ctx, `SELECT variant_position, 
         +group_position, channel, status,                               
      34 +                count(*), count(DISTINCT customer_id), COALESCE
         +(sum(GREATEST(attempts - 1, 0)), 0)

         +nel, status

      303 +func TestBehavioralGroups(t *testing.T) {                     
      304 +  ctx, pool, seg := fixture(t)                                
      305 +  svc := newSvc(pool, Mock{}, seg)

          +Invalid) {

          +r != nil {

⏺ All five phases are implemented as planned, and the Standard flow kept working
  throughout. The Go tests pass, the frontend type-checks and builds, and the
  main flows worked against real Claude through the running backend. I did no
  browser testing, so the new UI hasn't been looked at in a browser yet.

  Language data
  - The existing database had every customer as Turkish, because the language
    column was added after seeding. On startup, a one-time backfill now changes
    only that column, deterministically: the dev database now has 3,560 Turkish,
    1,199 English and 241 German customers.
  - The seed now produces the same mix.
  - German is deliberately unsupported, so those customers show the rule: anyone
    outside Turkish/English gets English.

  Drafts and variants
  - Drafts are unlimited, can be written by hand or duplicated, and record where
    they came from. Nothing ever overwrites or deletes one.
  - A/B variants are drafts promoted explicitly, 2–3 at most. Replacing,
    removing and ending the test only change which drafts are variants.
  - "Generate candidate" writes one new draft per call with a deliberately
    different approach. It stays an ordinary draft until you promote it.
  - Each customer is assigned to a variant from a hash of campaign and customer,
    so they get the same variant for SMS and email, and on every retry.

  Localization
  - Drafts are written in the audience's most common language, then
    automatically translated into the other languages present.
  - A translation is marked out of date when its source text changes, and can be
    updated or edited by hand. Missing translations block approval.

  Behavioral groups
  - One Claude call proposes groups from audience statistics only. The backend
    validates the rules with the existing audience-rule validator and assigns
    customers in SQL: first matching group wins, the rest form "Everyone else".
    Groups therefore never overlap or lose anyone.
  - Proposed groups with unusable rules or no members are dropped with a
    warning. If no meaningful split exists, the panel says so and you continue
    with Standard.
  - Before generating, you see a cost estimate. Generation is one call per
    group, covering only the languages in that group.
  - Each group shows three representative customers: a typical one, the
    highest-value one, and the one with the least history.

  Individual personalization
  - You generate a sample for one customer and see exactly what was sent to
    Claude: first name, city and order/cart activity, never email, phone or last
    name. A cost estimate follows.
  - Nothing runs for everyone until you confirm "Apply to all". It then runs in
    the background with at most 4 calls at a time and can be stopped and
    resumed.
  - Customers with no history get the base draft without a call. A message that
    fails validation or mentions an offer not in the brief is rejected, and that
    customer also gets the base draft.

  Cost
  - Every AI call is recorded, and the cost shown is the actual total so far,
    kept separate from delivery cost.
  - Estimates use earlier calls when available and say when the final cost may
    differ.

  Consistency checks and approval
  - Blocking: invalid content in any variant, group or language; missing
    translations; incomplete groups; individual generation still pending; and a
    channel nobody in the audience can receive.
  - Warnings only: a first-purchase message going to people who already ordered,
    or a "welcome back" message going to people who never did. Each text is
    checked against the customers who actually receive it.
  - Approval freezes the whole configuration, and every later change
    returns 409. Nothing is sent.

  Monitoring contract
  - A per-recipient resolver works out each customer's final message, variant,
    group, language and source. The review screen's counts and message previews
    use it.
  - A deliveries table and a breakdown query (by variant, group, channel and
    status, with retries) define what the delivery pipeline will fill in. There
    is no worker or dashboard yet, as agreed.

  Checks run
  - Go tests cover:
    - drafts never altered;
    - variant limits and replacement;
    - stable, even assignment over 3,000 customers;
    - language fallback;
    - groups exclusive and complete;
    - only allowed customer data sent, and no-history fallback;
    - invented offers rejected;
    - concurrency staying at or under 4;
    - the checks before approval;
    - everything immutable after approval;
    - breakdown totals matching stored records.
  - Real Claude on Haiku:
    - A Turkish brief produced Turkish and English drafts that both kept the 30%
      offer.
    - Claude proposed four sensible groups covering all 151 reachable customers,
      and their messages fit them. For example, the never-ordered group got
      "your first order". Generation cost about $0.0025.
    - One individual sample for Kerem used the books actually in his cart.
      Applying to the remaining 150 was estimated at about $0.03. I didn't run
      the bulk generation against the real API.

  Fixes made along the way
  - Claude was echoing the brief's style note ("A friendly reminder!") into
    messages. I added a prompt rule against it, but older drafts and samples
    still carry the phrase.
  - Go's case-insensitive matching doesn't treat Turkish "İ" as "i", which broke
    the first-purchase check for Turkish text.

  Remaining limitations (also in the README)
  - Generation jobs and locks live in memory, so this assumes a single API
    instance. After a restart, Resume continues pending individual messages.
  - The consistency checks are a few keyword rules.
  - There are no delivery workers or dashboards yet, and monitoring will report
    delivery results only: no conversion metrics and no automatic winner.
  - In-progress campaigns are saved, but there is no list or link to resume them
    after a page reload.
  - The dev database now holds test campaigns 1–3 from these checks.

  Nothing is committed.

⏺ All the feedback is addressed. The Go tests, frontend type-check and build
  pass, and the backend is healthy after the reseed. I haven't looked at the new
  screens in a browser, so the step layout and the new loading states are
  untested visually.

  Steps instead of stacked panels. After choosing the audience, the composer is
  now Content → Strategy → Review & approve, with Back/Continue buttons. You can
  also click completed steps in the progress bar. Strategy is a single choice
  between Single message, A/B test, Behavioral groups and Individual, so it's
  clear these are alternatives. Switching never deletes drafts. If an A/B test
  is running, switching away asks for confirmation before ending it. The old
  "A/B works with Standard only" explanation is gone.

  Candidate flow (A/B). While Claude writes a candidate, its box shows a spinner
  and the SMS and subject appearing live, with a Stop button. The other
  controls are disabled. When it finishes, the box shows the new draft with
  three buttons: "Add as Variant B/C" (the variant list updates immediately),
  "Edit first" (opens it in Content) and "Not now". Errors appear inside the
  box.

  Review. "Check again" is gone. The review runs when you open the step and
  again about 0.6 s after any change. While it updates, the results dim and an
  "Updating…" spinner shows, and Approve stays disabled until it finishes.

  Behavioral groups. Each group now shows one typical customer: the one with the
  median spend.

  Copy. Info boxes are down to a line or two. Every AI cost figure is shown as
  "≈ $X projected", including the running total in the review.

  Seed data. Language is now chosen before the name, and names come from a pool
  for that language. About 1 in 1,000 English or German speakers keeps a Turkish
  name, and a test enforces both rules. The earlier startup backfill is
  removed. As you asked, I wiped the customers, orders and carts, and my test
  campaigns with them, then let the server reseed:

  ┌──────────┬───────┬───────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Language │ Share │                Example                │
  ├──────────┼───────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Turkish  │ 77.3% │ —                                     │
  ├──────────┼───────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ English  │ 19.9% │ —                                     │
  ├──────────┼───────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ German   │ 2.9%  │ Marie Müller, mariemuller@example.org │
  └──────────┴───────┴───────────────────────────────────────┘

  Only 1 of 1,136 English or German speakers has a Turkish name.

  Nothing is committed.
